# Part 3 evidence ledger (Claude Code session)

**Date:** 2026-09-29 · **Session:** Claude Code (Part 3 research, in parallel with the Codex session) · **Scope:** the evidence behind `notes/part3/2026-09-29-part3-plan-claude-zh.md` (the plan draft) and `research/2026-09-29-part3-codex-report-review.md` (the review of the Codex plan report). New IDs continue the repository maxima E323 and D320: E324–E643 here; D321–D335 are in the review file; D336–D435 here.

## How the evidence was produced

Edwin's rule of 2026-09-29: a source is evidence only if its full text was read and each statement is locked to a specific sentence or table row. The steps, with the scripts in `tools/evidence-check/`:

1. **Search.** Three sweeps by independent finders: 11 lanes (records w), 6 lanes added after Edwin's clarifications (n), and 10 gaps named by critics of the first drafts (g). Finders' reports were leads only. Two further batches were read on 2026-09-29: 34 close works that the Codex survey and the deck name and the store lacked (h), and supplementary items for the 23 works the Codex plan report cites (x).
2. **Read.** One reader per work saved the full text (arXiv HTML or PDF text, publisher PDF, or the official page) and wrote a record of up to 10 items (up to 16 in batch x). Each item carries the claim, the figure as printed, the measurement definition, the location, the line number in the saved text and a verbatim anchor.
3. **Mechanical check.** A script checked that each anchor occurs at its line and that the item's numbers stand within three lines of it.
4. **Audit.** A second reader, who did not write the record, tried to refute each item against the saved text. Items the auditor corrected appear with the corrected wording.
5. **Source rule.** Each work was screened under the rule of 2026-09-29 (D313): formally published top venues first (venue and track confirmed at the venue's own page), then official technical material of leading AI companies, then preprints from strong universities and established research laboratories. An acceptance reported only by the authors is not counted as publication.
6. **Use.** Only items that passed steps 3 and 4, from works that passed step 5, are cited. Digests, the plan and the review were each checked by script (numbers against the cited items) and by independent reviewers.

**Counts.** 345 records were written for 314 distinct works read in full. 276 records (247 distinct works) are eligible under the source rule and passed the audit: 106 works at formally confirmed top venues, 9 official materials of leading companies, 132 institutional preprints. They hold 2,923 audited items. 67 records are held or excluded by the source rule and are not used. 116 further works reported by the finders were never read and are not used. The documents of this session cite 320 of the items, which receive the E-IDs below.

**Quotations.** This file and the records files give the location of every item (section, table, line of the saved text) and no verbatim text of the sources. The anchors are in the verified store, outside the repository. The repository rule asks for a verbatim quote in verification tables; whether locations are enough is a decision for Edwin (section 4).

**Not done.** No experiment was reproduced. No budget was computed. The slides, the canonical dossier and the Codex files were not changed.

## 2 D-ledger additions

The entries below were drafted from the first two batches of records and then audited one by one against both places they compare (the ledger line or the first place in the source, and the second place in the source). [A] = conflict between the ledger and a source; [B] = conflict inside a source; [C] = version or venue note. Where the auditor corrected the resolution, the corrected text is shown. Replacement wording for ledger lines is in the Resolution column.

| ID | Work | The ledger or the source says (place A) | The source says (place B; location, version) | Resolution | Source (read 2026-09-29) | Items | Audit | Source class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D336 | [A] ActionEngine (w016) | E226 (part2-acceleration-by-term.md l.1018) and dossier §3.1 row (l.468): WebArena Reddit, 106 tasks, AgentOccam on GPT-4-Turbo vs ActionEngine on Claude Sonnet 4.5: success 66→95%, 237→118 s, $0.71→$0.06 per task, 10.2→1.8 LLM calls; flagged model-confounded. D296 (citation-tiers.md l.239) notes only that v2 has new results (655 tasks, 91.2%) and that the deck keeps v1. | v2 (28 Sep 2026) runs every method on Claude Opus 4.6 (l.320). Table 1 (l.333-387), Reddit 106 tasks: AgentOccam 76.2%, Claude Code 92.4%, ActionEngine 95.0%; latency 536 / 59 / 18 s. All 655 tasks: 56.8 / 82.7 / 91.2%; 787 / 87 / 27 s. Cost $0.34 / $0.40 / $0.05 per task at $5/$25 per M tokens, with prompt/prefix caching (l.402-405). LLM calls 7–16 vs 1.5–3.5 per task (l.398). None of the v1 Reddit figures appear in v2. | E226 is correct for v1 only; keep citing v1 for those numbers. Add v2 as a separate row. In v2 every method runs on Claude Opus 4.6. On Reddit, AgentOccam 76.2 %, Claude Code 92.4 %, ActionEngine 95.0 %, so ActionEngine gains 18.8 pp over AgentOccam and 2.6 pp over Claude Code (both calc.). v2 also scores differently from v1: failed tasks were reviewed by hand and format-only mismatches count as successes. v2 uses an SMG refined during warm-up. So the move from 66 % to 76.2 % for AgentOccam is not a model effect alone. ActionEngine's Reddit latency is 118 s in v1 and 18 s in v2. The v2 costs ($0.34 / $0.40 / $0.05) are averages over all four domains. ActionEngine's Reddit-only cost in v2 is $0.03 with caching and $0.11 without (Table 2b). All costs are estimated from tokens, per attempt. ## 3 E-ledger patches

### 3a Replacement text for existing lines

Replacement wording for existing ledger lines is given in the Resolution column of the [A] entries of section 2 and in section 3 of `research/2026-09-29-part3-codex-report-review.md`. The canonical dossier is not changed.

### 3b New E-entries

Entries E324–E643. Each is one audited item of the verified store; the P3 record column gives the item, whose full record is in the records file of its batch.

| ID | Figure | Measurement definition and conditions | Source (URL, version, location) | Date | Label | Found by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E324 | **In standard AWM, induced workflows are added to the agent's original memory as auxiliary memory (M + W -> M_w), and the LM backbone still produces the actions from the instruction, the workflow-augmented memory and the observation, L(q, M+W, o) -> a. Footnote 1 (line 104) says memory is usually implemented as a system prompt or auxiliary information in the main prompt context.** | Method description; no measurement. Unit of generation: one action produced by the LM per observe-act step (the loop of Sec. 2.1, line 105). | https://arxiv.org/html/2409.07429v1, arXiv:2409.07429v1 (arXiv HTML rendering saved as text); Sec. 2.3 Inducing and Using Workflows (lines 127-128); footnote 1 in Sec. 2.1 (line 104); line 127 of the saved text | 2024-09-11 | Primary, published: Proceedings of the 42nd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2025), PMLR 26… | P3 record x010#0 | audited: supported |
| E325 | **AWM_AS wraps each workflow as a high-level action that runs a pre-determined series of primitive actions; the agent may call primitive or workflow actions at each step. On Mind2Web (gpt-4, Table 9) it raises step SR by 1.3 points over memory-only AWM (46.4 vs 45.1, lines 516 and 521), and agents called workflow actions in only 18.5% of tasks. For task SR the text says AWM_AS gets 'the same overall success rate, 3.2', whereas Table 9 prints 3.6 for AWM_AS and 4.8 for AWM (line 517).** (+1.3 step SR; task SR 3.2 in text vs 3.6 in table; workflow actions called in 18.5 % of tasks) | Mind2Web step SR and task SR (%) with gpt-4; the 18.5% is the share of tasks in which agent predictions call a workflow action, from the authors' analysis of predictions. | https://arxiv.org/html/2409.07429v1, arXiv:2409.07429v1 (arXiv HTML rendering saved as text); Sec. 5 Exploring Workflow Utilization in Context and in Action, Table 9 and text (lines 497-526); line 525 of the saved text | 2024-09-11 | Primary, published: Proceedings of the 42nd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2025), PMLR 26… | P3 record x010#8 | audited: supported |
| E326 | **A workflow is an NL description d (a summary of its function, extracted heuristically from instructions or summarized by an LM) plus a series of steps. Each step has (1) an NL description of the current environment state, e.g. 'Order {id} is shown', (2) the agent's reasoning, and (3) an action written as an executable program over the environment, e.g. stop(). ## 4 Still open

- **Decision for Edwin: quotations.** Locations only, or verbatim quotes as the repository rule asks.
- **Decision for Edwin: SAP Concur product documentation.** Ten records of SAP help and developer pages (g001–g009, g040) were read and audited, and the source rule excludes them as product documentation of an ordinary company. The plan's question of where the loop can run (test users, test entity, clean-up, irreversible steps, the duplicate-expense rule) can only be answered from such pages. They are listed in section 1b and not used.
- **Held works.** Works held by the source rule (section 1b) can be used once a formal venue is confirmed or an eligible institution appears on the title page. Those closest to the plan: PRISM (prompt versus middleware routing with an equal budget), REBASE (stale experience after app updates), "Where Does Harness-Optimization Value Live?".
- **Unverified leads.** Section 1d lists works the finders reported that were never read. None is used.
- **Unresolved inside sources.** The [B] entries of section 2 whose resolution says "unresolved" record numbers that disagree inside one paper. They are cited, if at all, with that caveat.
- **Figures in images.** Values that a paper shows only in a figure are not in the saved texts and were not used, except where an item says the value was read from a figure caption or text.
- **Merging.** E-entries and D-entries of this file, of the review file and of the Codex files (E304–E323, D311–D320) are proposed records for v3.1. The canonical v3 is unchanged.
